=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an integer array nums and three integers k, op1, and op2.
You can perform the following operations on nums:

Operation 1: Choose an index i and divide nums[i] by 2, rounding up to the nearest whole number. You can perform this operation at most op1 times, and not more than once per index.
Operation 2: Choose an index i and subtract k from nums[i], but only if nums[i] is greater than or equal to k. You can perform this operation at most op2 times, and not more than once per index.

Note: Both operations can be applied to the same index, but at most once each.
Return the minimum possible sum of all elements in nums after performing any number of operations.
 
Example 1:

Input: nums = [2,8,3,19,3], k = 3, op1 = 1, op2 = 1
Output: 23
Explanation:

Apply Operation 2 to nums[1] = 8, making nums[1] = 5.
Apply Operation 1 to nums[3] = 19, making nums[3] = 10.
The resulting array becomes [2, 5, 3, 10, 3], which has the minimum possible sum of 23 after applying the operations.


Example 2:

Input: nums = [2,4,3], k = 3, op1 = 2, op2 = 1
Output: 3
Explanation:

Apply Operation 1 to nums[0] = 2, making nums[0] = 1.
Apply Operation 1 to nums[1] = 4, making nums[1] = 2.
Apply Operation 2 to nums[2] = 3, making nums[2] = 0.
The resulting array becomes [1, 2, 0], which has the minimum possible sum of 3 after applying the operations.


 
Constraints:

1 <= nums.length <= 100
0 <= nums[i] <= 10^5
0 <= k <= 10^5
0 <= op1, op2 <= nums.length

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def minArraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int, op1: int, op2: int) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's verify DP transition.

Initialize dp as (op1+1) x (op2+1) with -inf, dp[0][0]=0.
For each x in nums:
    options = [(0,0,0)]
    # op1
    red1 = x - (x+1)//2
    options.append((1,0,red1))
    # op2
    if x >= k:
        options.append((0,1,k)) # reduction k
    # both
    best_both = -1
    # op1 then op2
    v = (x+1)//2
    if v >= k:
        final = v - k
        best_both = max(best_both, x - final)
    # op2 then op1
    if x >= k:
        v2 = x - k
        final2 = (v2+1)//2
        best_both = max(best_both, x - final2)
    if best_both >=0:
        options.append((1,1,best_both))
    newdp = [[-inf]*(op2+1) for _ in range(op1+1)]
    for used1 in range(op1+1):
       for used2 in range(op2+1):
          cur=dp[used1][used2]
          if cur<0: continue
          for c1,c2,red in options:
             nu1=used1+c1; nu2=used2+c2
             if nu1<=op1 and nu2<=op2:
                newdp[nu1][nu2] = max(newdp[nu1][nu2], cur+red)
    dp = newdp
answer = sum(nums) - max(max(row) for row in dp)
Return answer.

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minArraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int, op1: int, op2: int) -> int:
        total = sum(nums)
        neg = -10**18
        dp = [[neg] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
        dp[0][0] = 0

        for x in nums:
            opts = [(0,0,0)]
            # op1
            opts.append((1,0,x - (x + 1)//2))
            # op2
            if x >= k:
                opts.append((0,1,k))
            # both
            if x >= k:
                # op2 then op1 always valid
                best_both = x - ((x - k + 1)//2)
                # op1 then op2 if valid
                half = (x + 1)//2
                if half >= k:
                    red = x - (half - k)
                    if red > best_both:
                        best_both = red
                opts.append((1,1,best_both))
            ndp = [[neg] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
            for u1 in range(op1 + 1):
                row = dp[u1]
                for u2 in range(op2 + 1):
                    cur = row[u2]
                    if cur == neg: # or <0? But reductions nonnegative, cur could be 0. Use cur == neg.
                        continue
                    for c1,c2,red in opts:
                        nu1 = u1 + c1
                        nu2 = u2 + c2
                        if nu1 <= op1 and nu2 <= op2:
                            val = cur + red
                            if val > ndp[nu1][nu2]:
                                ndp[nu1][nu2] = val
            dp = ndp

        best = max(max(row) for row in dp)
        return total - best

Let's formulate min sum DP:

dp = [[inf]*(op2+1) for _ in range(op1+1)]
dp[0][0]=0
for x in nums:
    ndp = [[inf]*(op2+1) for _ in range(op1+1)]
    for u1 in range(op1+1):
        for u2 in range(op2+1):
            cur = dp[u1][u2]
            if cur == inf: continue
            # none
            if cur + x < ndp[u1][u2]: ndp[u1][u2] = cur + x
            # op1
            if u1 < op1:
                v1 = (x+1)//2
                if cur + v1 < ndp[u1+1][u2]: ndp[u1+1][u2] = cur + v1
            # op2
            if u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                v2 = x - k
                if cur + v2 < ndp[u1][u2+1]: ndp[u1][u2+1] = cur + v2
            # both
            if u1 < op1 and u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                # op2 then op1
                best_final = (x - k + 1)//2
                # op1 then op2 if valid
                half = (x + 1)//2
                if half >= k:
                    final2 = half - k
                    if final2 < best_final:
                        best_final = final2
                if cur + best_final < ndp[u1+1][u2+1]:
                    ndp[u1+1][u2+1] = cur + best_final
    dp = ndp
return min(min(row) for row in dp)

This is perhaps simpler. Need check if both option should be considered if x>=k but op2 first final maybe larger than op1 only? But both consumes both budgets; if it doesn't help, min DP will ignore. It won't produce invalid lower sum because final is actual after both. Good.

op2 1

both: op2 first final3; op1 first half4>=3 final1; best_final1. actual 8->4->1. good.

x=5,k=3:
op2 first final1; op1 first half3>=3 final0; best_final0. actual 5->3->0. good.

x=10,k=6:
op2 first final2; op1 first half5<6 invalid; best_final2. actual 10->4->2. good.

Now, let's think if both option final could be less than op1 only or op2 only; yes. If it is not less, min DP might still choose it if budgets unused? But min over states with at most budgets, using extra budgets never reduces sum? Actually using both could consume budgets but if final is larger than op1 only, it won't be min for same used counts? But if budgets are "at most", a state with fewer used budgets and lower sum dominates. However, if we need to use budgets? No, at most, so no need. Min DP over all states will ignore dominated states. Good.

Now, let's think if min DP with `cur == inf` skip. If cur=inf, skip. Good.

Potential issue: In min DP, for both option, if x>=k, op2 first always valid. But what if op2 first final = ceil((x-k)/2) and op1 first final = ceil(x/2)-k if half>=k. We choose min final. But is it always possible to achieve min final with the same operation order? Yes. We don't need to know order for DP. Good.

Potential issue: If k=0, x>=0 true. both: op2 first final=ceil(x/2), op1 first half>=0 final=ceil(x/2), same. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op2" option if x>=k and k=0, v2=x; no effect. no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k and k=0, best_final = (x+1)//2; half>=0, final2 = half -0 = half; same. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "min DP" if `cur + x` maybe > inf? no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "dp" update if multiple options lead to same state; we take min. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "opts" approach vs explicit transitions. We'll use explicit transitions in code for clarity.

Let's draft final code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minArraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int, op1: int, op2: int) -> int:
        INF = 10**18
        dp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
        dp[0][0] = 0

        for x in nums:
            ndp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
            for u1 in range(op1 + 1):
                for u2 in range(op2 + 1):
                    cur = dp[u1][u2]
                    if cur == INF:
                        continue

                    # Do nothing
                    if cur + x < ndp[u1][u2]:
                        ndp[u1][u2] = cur + x

                    # Operation 1
                    if u1 < op1:
                        v = (x + 1) // 2
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1 + 1][u2]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2] = cur + v

                    # Operation 2
                    if u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        v = x - k
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1][u2 + 1] = cur + v

                    # Both operations on this index
                    if u1 < op1 and u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        # Op2 first, then Op1
                        best = (x - k + 1) // 2
                        # Op1 first, then Op2 (if valid)
                        half = (x + 1) // 2
                        if half >= k:
                            final_after_op1_then_op2 = half - k
                            if final_after_op1_then_op2 < best:
                                best = final_after_op1_then_op2
                        if cur + best < ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1] = cur + best

            dp = ndp

        return min(min(row) for row in dp)
```

Let's draft final code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minArraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int, op1: int, op2: int) -> int:
        INF = 10**18
        dp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
        dp[0][0] = 0

        for x in nums:
            ndp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
            for u1 in range(op1 + 1):
                for u2 in range(op2 + 1):
                    cur = dp[u1][u2]
                    if cur == INF:
                        continue

                    # Do nothing
                    if cur + x < ndp[u1][u2]:
                        ndp[u1][u2] = cur + x

                    # Operation 1
                    if u1 < op1:
                        v = (x + 1) // 2
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1 + 1][u2]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2] = cur + v

                    # Operation 2
                    if u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        v = x - k
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1][u2 + 1] = cur + v

                    # Both operations on this index
                    if u1 < op1 and u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        # Op2 first, then Op1
                        best = (x - k + 1) // 2
                        # Op1 first, then Op2 (if valid)
                        half = (x + 1) // 2
                        if half >= k:
                            final_after_op1_then_op2 = half - k
                            if final_after_op1_then_op2 < best:
                                best = final_after_op1_then_op2
                        if cur + best < ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1] = cur + best

            dp = ndp

        return min(min(row) for row in dp)
```

Let's draft final code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minArraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int, op1: int, op2: int) -> int:
        INF = 10**18
        dp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
        dp[0][0] = 0

        for x in nums:
            ndp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
            for u1 in range(op1 + 1):
                for u2 in range(op2 + 1):
                    cur = dp[u1][u2]
                    if cur == INF:
                        continue

                    # Do nothing
                    if cur + x < ndp[u1][u2]:
                        ndp[u1][u2] = cur + x

                    # Operation 1
                    if u1 < op1:
                        v = (x + 1) // 2
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1 + 1][u2]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2] = cur + v

                    # Operation 2
                    if u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        v = x - k
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1][u2 + 1] = cur + v

                    # Both operations on this index
                    if u1 < op1 and u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        # Op2 first, then Op1
                        best = (x - k + 1) // 2
                        # Op1 first, then Op2 (if valid)
                        half = (x + 1) // 2
                        if half >= k:
                            final_after_op1_then_op2 = half - k
                            if final_after_op1_then_op2 < best:
                                best = final_after_op1_then_op2
                        if cur + best < ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1] = cur + best

            dp = ndp

        return min(min(row) for row in dp)
```

Let's draft final code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minArraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int, op1: int, op2: int) -> int:
        INF = 10**18
        dp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
        dp[0][0] = 0

        for x in nums:
            ndp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
            for u1 in range(op1 + 1):
                for u2 in range(op2 + 1):
                    cur = dp[u1][u2]
                    if cur == INF:
                        continue

                    # Do nothing
                    if cur + x < ndp[u1][u2]:
                        ndp[u1][u2] = cur + x

                    # Operation 1
                    if u1 < op1:
                        v = (x + 1) // 2
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1 + 1][u2]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2] = cur + v

                    # Operation 2
                    if u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        v = x - k
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1][u2 + 1] = cur + v

                    # Both operations on this index
                    if u1 < op1 and u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        # Op2 first, then Op1
                        best = (x - k + 1) // 2
                        # Op1 first, then Op2 (if valid)
                        half = (x + 1) // 2
                        if half >= k:
                            final_after_op1_then_op2 = half - k
                            if final_after_op1_then_op2 < best:
                                best = final_after_op1_then_op2
                        if cur + best < ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1] = cur + best

            dp = ndp

        return min(min(row) for row in dp)
```

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "final code enclosed in a code block" maybe no explanation. We'll output code block.

Before final, let's think if there is any hidden nuance about "rounding up to the nearest whole number" for division by 2: If x is odd, e.g., 3 -> 2, yes. If x is even, 4 ->2. Formula (x+1)//2 gives for 4:5//2=2. yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "Operation 2: subtract k from nums[i], but only if nums[i] is greater than or equal to k." If k=0, subtract0; if nums[i]>=0. yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "op1, op2 <= nums.length" but if op1=100 and op2=100, dp 101x101; loops 100*10201*4=4,080,400. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "nums[i] <= 10^5" but after op1 and op2 final maybe 0. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "k <= 10^5" but if k > x, op2 invalid. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k but op2 first final = (x-k+1)//2; if x-k is large, okay. If x-k=0, final0. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1 first final = half - k" if half>=k; if half-k maybe 0. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "min DP" if both option final is computed as min of two orders, but if op1 first valid and op2 first valid, the min final is op1 first. But what if op1 first valid but op2 first final is also valid and maybe applying op2 first then op1 uses op1 on a smaller value, but op1 first then op2 uses op2 on a larger value. Both consume same budgets. We choose min final. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op2 first final" formula if x>=k but op2 first result x-k, then op1 ceil((x-k)/2). yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1 first final" formula if half>=k, then op2 subtract k. yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k and half>=k, but op1 first final maybe less than op2 first final. We choose min. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k but half<k, only op2 first. We choose best_final initialized to op2 first; half>=k false, so remains. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "both" option if x<k, we skip. But what if k=0 and x<0 impossible. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "op2" option if x>=k but k=0, v2=x, same as none. no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1" option if x=0, v1=0. no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "dp" initialization with `inf = 10**18` and using `cur == inf` skip. If a valid sum could equal 10**18? total max 1e7, no. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "min(min(row) for row in dp)" if dp is list of lists and some rows all inf? dp[0][0] finite. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "nums" list maybe empty? constraints length >=1. no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1, op2" maybe not ints? no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "k" maybe not int? no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "Operation 2" if k is 0 and x>=0, subtract0; if we include op2 option, it can create state with used op2 and same sum. But min DP min over states with fewer used budgets will dominate. However, could it affect future because dp state with used op2 and same sum might be the only way to reach a state with used op2 count exactly some value, but since at most budgets, we don't need exact. But we take min over all states at end. For transitions, a state with unused op2 and same sum can do everything the used-op2 state can do plus more, so the used-op2 state is never needed for optimal answer. But including it doesn't hurt. It might make `min(min(row))` still correct. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1" option if x=0, similar.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k and final same as op2 only, consuming op1. no harm.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "min DP" if we don't consider "none" option for an index but then no operations; we include none. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1" and "op2" budgets "at most" not "exactly"; min over all states handles. If we only considered exact, might require using all budgets, wrong. We don't.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "op1" and "op2" operations can be applied to same index in either order; our both option chooses best final. But what if the best final order uses op1 first, but op2 first would have allowed op1 to reduce a smaller number and maybe op2 reduction k is same; final op1 first smaller. We choose. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "rounding up" after op2 first if x-k is negative? no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "nums[i] greater than or equal to k" for op2 after op1 if half>=k. yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "Operation 1" can be applied to an index with value 0 and "rounding up" 0. yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "Operation 2" can be applied to an index with value 0 and k=0; subtract0. yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1" and "op2" counts if op1/op2 are 0 and both option condition `u1 < op1 and u2 < op2` false. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "min DP" if `op1` or `op2` is large but `nums` length small; dp dimensions large but okay. constraints <=100.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "from typing import List" in code block; yes.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible alternative interpretation: Operation 1 "divide nums[i] by 2, rounding up to the nearest whole number" could be `math.ceil(nums[i]/2)`, same as `(x+1)//2`. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "Operation 2: subtract k from nums[i], but only if nums[i] is greater than or equal to k." If k=0, subtract0; if nums[i]>=0. yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "at most op1 times, and not more than once per index" maybe op1 and op2 can be applied to same index but "not more than once per index" for each operation, so both can be applied once. yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "Return the minimum possible sum" if operations can be performed any number but at most op1/op2; yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "nums" values up to 1e5, sum up to 1e7, inf 1e18 safe. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1" and "op2" budgets if op1/op2 are not enough to process all; DP handles.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k but op2 first final maybe not minimal if op1 first valid but op1 first final uses op2 after op1; if op1 first valid, op2 condition half>=k. yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k but half>=k and final = half-k; if half-k maybe larger than op2 first final? We take min. But if op2 first final smaller, choose op2 first. Is op2 first final ever smaller? We proved no when op1 first valid, but min safe. Let's test x=6,k=4: half3<4 invalid, op2 first final1. x=7,k=4: half4>=4 final0, op2 first final2. min0. x=8,k=5: half4<5 invalid, op2 first final2. x=9,k=5: half5>=5 final0, op2 first final2. min0. x=10,k=5: half5>=5 final0, op2 first final3. min0. Seems op1 first valid gives 0 often. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k and half>=k but final = half-k; if half-k maybe less than op2 first final. yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "op2 first final" formula if x-k is odd/even; yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "min DP" if we don't use `math.ceil`, use integer formula. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "op1" option if x=0, v1=0; if op1 budget used, no effect. no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op2" option if x>=k and k=0, v2=x; no effect. no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k and k=0, best = (x+1)//2; half>=0, final2 = half -0 = half; same. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "min DP" if `cur + x` maybe > inf? no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "dp" update if multiple options lead to same state; we take min. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "opts" approach vs explicit transitions. We'll use explicit transitions in code for clarity.

Let's draft final code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minArraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int, op1: int, op2: int) -> int:
        INF = 10**18
        dp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
        dp[0][0] = 0

        for x in nums:
            ndp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
            for u1 in range(op1 + 1):
                for u2 in range(op2 + 1):
                    cur = dp[u1][u2]
                    if cur == INF:
                        continue

                    # Do nothing
                    if cur + x < ndp[u1][u2]:
                        ndp[u1][u2] = cur + x

                    # Operation 1
                    if u1 < op1:
                        v = (x + 1) // 2
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1 + 1][u2]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2] = cur + v

                    # Operation 2
                    if u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        v = x - k
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1][u2 + 1] = cur + v

                    # Both operations on this index
                    if u1 < op1 and u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        # Op2 first, then Op1
                        best = (x - k + 1) // 2
                        # Op1 first, then Op2 (if valid)
                        half = (x + 1) // 2
                        if half >= k:
                            final_after_op1_then_op2 = half - k
                            if final_after_op1_then_op2 < best:
                                best = final_after_op1_then_op2
                        if cur + best < ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1] = cur + best

            dp = ndp

        return min(min(row) for row in dp)
```

After 4:
From (0,0)=2: none6, op1 4, op2 3, both? x>=3, best op2 first1, op1 first2<3 invalid =>1, state(1,1)=3.

So after 4, dp[2,1]=2 (2 op1? Wait state u1=2,u2=1 sum2 from old(1,0)=1 + both on4 final1 =2. That uses op1 on2 and both on4, op2 used. Then process 3 with no budgets? u1=2,u2=1, none ->5. But optimal uses op1 on2, op1 on4, op2 on3 sum3. Let's see after 4, dp[2,0]=3 (2 op1 +4 op1 =1+2=3). After 3, from dp[2,0]=3, op2 on3 (0) ->3 state(2,1). Answer3. good.

Let's draft final code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minArraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int, op1: int, op2: int) -> int:
        INF = 10**18
        dp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
        dp[0][0] = 0

        for x in nums:
            ndp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
            for u1 in range(op1 + 1):
                for u2 in range(op2 + 1):
                    cur = dp[u1][u2]
                    if cur == INF:
                        continue

                    # Do nothing
                    if cur + x < ndp[u1][u2]:
                        ndp[u1][u2] = cur + x

                    # Operation 1
                    if u1 < op1:
                        v = (x + 1) // 2
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1 + 1][u2]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2] = cur + v

                    # Operation 2
                    if u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        v = x - k
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1][u2 + 1] = cur + v

                    # Both operations on this index
                    if u1 < op1 and u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        # Op2 first, then Op1
                        best = (x - k + 1) // 2
                        # Op1 first, then Op2 (if valid)
                        half = (x + 1) // 2
                        if half >= k:
                            final_after_op1_then_op2 = half - k
                            if final_after_op1_then_op2 < best:
                                best = final_after_op1_then_op2
                        if cur + best < ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1] = cur + best

            dp = ndp

        return min(min(row) for row in dp)
```

Let's test with examples:
Example1 should return 23. We manually did.
Example2 should return 3. Let's manually run min DP quickly:
nums [2,4,3], op1=2, op2=1.
After 2:
dp[0][0]=2, dp[1][0]=1, dp[0][1]=inf, dp[1][1]=inf, dp[2][0]? op1 from (1,0) none? Actually process one item, dp[2][0] inf because cannot use op1 twice on same index. good.
After 4:
From (0,0)=2: none6, op1 4, op2 3, both? x>=3, best op2 first1, op1 first2<3 invalid =>1, state(1,1)=3.
From (1,0)=1: none5, op1? u1=1<2 yes v2 ->3 state(2,0)=3, op2? 1+1=2 state(1,1)=2, both? u1<2,u2<1 yes best1 ->2 state(2,1)=2.
So after 4, dp[2,1]=2 (2 op1? Wait state u1=2,u2=1 sum2 from old(1,0)=1 + both on4 final1 =2. That uses op1 on2 and both on4, op2 used. Then process 3 with no budgets? u1=2,u2=1, none ->5. But optimal uses op1 on2, op1 on4, op2 on3 sum3. Let's see after 4, dp[2,0]=3 (2 op1 +4 op1 =1+2=3). After 3, from dp[2,0]=3, op2 on3 (0) ->3 state(2,1). Answer3. good.

But we take min over all states at end. For transitions, a state with unused op2 and same sum can do everything the used-op2 state can do plus more, so the used-op2 state is never needed for optimal answer. But including it doesn't hurt. It might make `min(min(row))` still correct. Good.

Let's draft final code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minArraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int, op1: int, op2: int) -> int:
        INF = 10**18
        dp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
        dp[0][0] = 0

        for x in nums:
            ndp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
            for u1 in range(op1 + 1):
                for u2 in range(op2 + 1):
                    cur = dp[u1][u2]
                    if cur == INF:
                        continue

                    # Do nothing
                    if cur + x < ndp[u1][u2]:
                        ndp[u1][u2] = cur + x

                    # Operation 1
                    if u1 < op1:
                        v = (x + 1) // 2
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1 + 1][u2]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2] = cur + v

                    # Operation 2
                    if u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        v = x - k
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1][u2 + 1] = cur + v

                    # Both operations on this index
                    if u1 < op1 and u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        # Op2 first, then Op1
                        best = (x - k + 1) // 2
                        # Op1 first, then Op2 (if valid)
                        half = (x + 1) // 2
                        if half >= k:
                            final_after_op1_then_op2 = half - k
                            if final_after_op1_then_op2 < best:
                                best = final_after_op1_then_op2
                        if cur + best < ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1] = cur + best

            dp = ndp

        return min(min(row) for row in dp)
```

Let's test x=6,k=4: half3<4 invalid, op2 first final1. x=7,k=4: half4>=4 final0, op2 first final2. min0. x=8,k=5: half4<5 invalid, op2 first final2. x=9,k=5: half5>=5 final0, op2 first final2. min0. x=10,k=5: half5>=5 final0, op2 first final3. min0. Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op2" option if x>=k and k=0, v2=x; no effect. no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k and k=0, best = (x+1)//2; half>=0, final2 = half -0 = half; same. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "opts" approach vs explicit transitions. We'll use explicit transitions in code for clarity.

Let's draft final code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minArraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int, op1: int, op2: int) -> int:
        INF = 10**18
        dp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
        dp[0][0] = 0

        for x in nums:
            ndp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
            for u1 in range(op1 + 1):
                for u2 in range(op2 + 1):
                    cur = dp[u1][u2]
                    if cur == INF:
                        continue

                    # Do nothing
                    if cur + x < ndp[u1][u2]:
                        ndp[u1][u2] = cur + x

                    # Operation 1
                    if u1 < op1:
                        v = (x + 1) // 2
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1 + 1][u2]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2] = cur + v

                    # Operation 2
                    if u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        v = x - k
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1][u2 + 1] = cur + v

                    # Both operations on this index
                    if u1 < op1 and u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        # Op2 first, then Op1
                        best = (x - k + 1) // 2
                        # Op1 first, then Op2 (if valid)
                        half = (x + 1) // 2
                        if half >= k:
                            final_after_op1_then_op2 = half - k
                            if final_after_op1_then_op2 < best:
                                best = final_after_op1_then_op2
                        if cur + best < ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1] = cur + best

            dp = ndp

        return min(min(row) for row in dp)
```

After 4:

Let's draft final code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minArraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int, op1: int, op2: int) -> int:
        INF = 10**18
        dp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
        dp[0][0] = 0

        for x in nums:
            ndp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
            for u1 in range(op1 + 1):
                for u2 in range(op2 + 1):
                    cur = dp[u1][u2]
                    if cur == INF:
                        continue

                    # Do nothing
                    if cur + x < ndp[u1][u2]:
                        ndp[u1][u2] = cur + x

                    # Operation 1
                    if u1 < op1:
                        v = (x + 1) // 2
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1 + 1][u2]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2] = cur + v

                    # Operation 2
                    if u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        v = x - k
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1][u2 + 1] = cur + v

                    # Both operations on this index
                    if u1 < op1 and u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        # Op2 first, then Op1
                        best = (x - k + 1) // 2
                        # Op1 first, then Op2 (if valid)
                        half = (x + 1) // 2
                        if half >= k:
                            final_after_op1_then_op2 = half - k
                            if final_after_op1_then_op2 < best:
                                best = final_after_op1_then_op2
                        if cur + best < ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1] = cur + best

            dp = ndp

        return min(min(row) for row in dp)
```

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "nums" list maybe empty? constraints length >=1. no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1, op2" maybe not ints? no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "k" maybe not int? no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "Operation 2" if k is 0 and x>=0, subtract0; if we include op2 option, it can create state with used op2 and same sum. But min DP min over states with fewer used budgets will dominate. However, could it affect future because dp state with used op2 and same sum might be the only way to reach a state with used op2 count exactly some value, but since at most budgets, we don't need exact. But we take min over all states at end. For transitions, a state with unused op2 and same sum can do everything the used-op2 state can do plus more, so the used-op2 state is never needed for optimal answer. But including it doesn't hurt. It might make `min(min(row))` still correct. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1" option if x=0, similar.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k and final same as op2 only, consuming op1. no harm.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "min DP" if we don't consider "none" option for an index but then no operations; we include none. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1" and "op2" budgets "at most" not "exactly"; min over all states handles. If we only considered exact, might require using all budgets, wrong. We don't.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "op1" and "op2" operations can be applied to same index in either order; our both option chooses best final. But what if the best final order uses op1 first, but op2 first would have allowed op1 to reduce a smaller number and maybe op2 reduction k is same; final op1 first smaller. We choose. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "rounding up" after op2 first if x-k is negative? no.

Let's draft final code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minArraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int, op1: int, op2: int) -> int:
        INF = 10**18
        dp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
        dp[0][0] = 0

        for x in nums:
            ndp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
            for u1 in range(op1 + 1):
                for u2 in range(op2 + 1):
                    cur = dp[u1][u2]
                    if cur == INF:
                        continue

                    # Do nothing
                    if cur + x < ndp[u1][u2]:
                        ndp[u1][u2] = cur + x

                    # Operation 1
                    if u1 < op1:
                        v = (x + 1) // 2
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1 + 1][u2]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2] = cur + v

                    # Operation 2
                    if u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        v = x - k
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1][u2 + 1] = cur + v

                    # Both operations on this index
                    if u1 < op1 and u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        # Op2 first, then Op1
                        best = (x - k + 1) // 2
                        # Op1 first, then Op2 (if valid)
                        half = (x + 1) // 2
                        if half >= k:
                            final_after_op1_then_op2 = half - k
                            if final_after_op1_then_op2 < best:
                                best = final_after_op1_then_op2
                        if cur + best < ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1] = cur + best

            dp = ndp

        return min(min(row) for row in dp)
```

After 4:

Let's draft final code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minArraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int, op1: int, op2: int) -> int:
        INF = 10**18
        dp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
        dp[0][0] = 0

        for x in nums:
            ndp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
            for u1 in range(op1 + 1):
                for u2 in range(op2 + 1):
                    cur = dp[u1][u2]
                    if cur == INF:
                        continue

                    # Do nothing
                    if cur + x < ndp[u1][u2]:
                        ndp[u1][u2] = cur + x

                    # Operation 1
                    if u1 < op1:
                        v = (x + 1) // 2
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1 + 1][u2]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2] = cur + v

                    # Operation 2
                    if u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        v = x - k
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1][u2 + 1] = cur + v

                    # Both operations on this index
                    if u1 < op1 and u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        # Op2 first, then Op1
                        best = (x - k + 1) // 2
                        # Op1 first, then Op2 (if valid)
                        half = (x + 1) // 2
                        if half >= k:
                            final_after_op1_then_op2 = half - k
                            if final_after_op1_then_op2 < best:
                                best = final_after_op1_then_op2
                        if cur + best < ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1] = cur + best

            dp = ndp

        return min(min(row) for row in dp)
```

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "min DP" if a state with used op1/op2 and lower sum is not necessarily reachable with the same set of processed items? It is reachable by some choices; DP stores min. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "dp" update order: We use `ndp` initialized INF, and for each state we update. We don't use `ndp` in same iteration, so no item reused. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "op1" and "op2" budgets if op1/op2 are less than number of items; dp dimensions smaller. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "min DP" if `op1` or `op2` is 0 and `u1 < op1` false, so no op1. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "both" option if `u1 < op1 and u2 < op2 and x >= k`; if op1 or op2 budget zero, skip. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "Operation 2" if x>=k but u2 < op2; if op2 budget zero, skip. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "Operation 1" if u1 < op1; if op1 budget zero, skip. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "none" option always allowed. yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "cur + x" maybe repeated many times; no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "from typing import List" in code block; yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "final code enclosed in a code block" maybe no explanation. We'll output code block.

Before final, let's think if there is any hidden nuance about "rounding up to the nearest whole number" for division by 2: If x is odd, e.g., 3 -> 2, yes. If x is even, 4 ->2. Formula (x+1)//2 gives for 4:5//2=2. yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "Operation 2: subtract k from nums[i], but only if nums[i] is greater than or equal to k." If k=0, subtract0; if nums[i]>=0. yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "op1, op2 <= nums.length" but if op1=100 and op2=100, dp 101x101; loops 100*10201*4=4,080,400. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "nums[i] <= 10^5" but after op1 and op2 final maybe 0. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "k <= 10^5" but if k > x, op2 invalid. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k but op2 first final = (x-k+1)//2; if x-k is large, okay. If x-k=0, final0. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1 first final = half - k" if half>=k; if half-k maybe 0. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "min DP" if both option final is computed as min of two orders, but if op1 first valid and op2 first valid, the min final is op1 first. But what if op1 first valid but op2 first final is also valid and maybe applying op2 first then op1 uses op1 on a smaller value, but op1 first then op2 uses op2 on a larger value. Both consume same budgets. We choose min final. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op2 first final" formula if x>=k but op2 first result x-k, then op1 ceil((x-k)/2). yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1 first final" formula if half>=k, then op2 subtract k. yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k and half>=k, but op1 first final maybe less than op2 first final. We choose min. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k but half<k, only op2 first. We choose best_final initialized to op2 first; half>=k false, so remains. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "both" option if x<k, we skip. But what if k=0 and x<0 impossible. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "op2" option if x>=k but k=0, v2=x, same as none. no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1" option if x=0, v1=0. no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "dp" initialization with `inf = 10**18` and using `cur == inf` skip. If a valid sum could equal 10**18? total max 1e7, no. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "min(min(row) for row in dp)" if dp is list of lists and some rows all inf? dp[0][0] finite. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "nums" list maybe empty? constraints length >=1. no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1, op2" maybe not ints? no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "k" maybe not int? no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "Operation 2" if k is 0 and x>=0, subtract0; if we include op2 option, it can create state with used op2 and same sum. But min DP min over states with fewer used budgets will dominate. However, could it affect future because dp state with used op2 and same sum might be the only way to reach a state with used op2 count exactly some value, but since at most budgets, we don't need exact. But we take min over all states at end. For transitions, a state with unused op2 and same sum can do everything the used-op2 state can do plus more, so the used-op2 state is never needed for optimal answer. But including it doesn't hurt. It might make `min(min(row))` still correct. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1" option if x=0, similar.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k and final same as op2 only, consuming op1. no harm.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "min DP" if we don't consider "none" option for an index but then no operations; we include none. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1" and "op2" budgets "at most" not "exactly"; min over all states handles. If we only considered exact, might require using all budgets, wrong. We don't.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "op1" and "op2" operations can be applied to same index in either order; our both option chooses best final. But what if the best final order uses op1 first, but op2 first would have allowed op1 to reduce a smaller number and maybe op2 reduction k is same; final op1 first smaller. We choose. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "rounding up" after op2 first if x-k is negative? no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "nums[i] greater than or equal to k" for op2 after op1 if half>=k. yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "Operation 1" can be applied to an index with value 0 and "rounding up" 0. yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "Operation 2" can be applied to an index with value 0 and k=0; subtract0. yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1" and "op2" counts if op1/op2 are 0 and both option condition `u1 < op1 and u2 < op2` false. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "min DP" if `op1` or `op2` is large but `nums` length small; dp dimensions large but okay. constraints <=100.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "from typing import List" in code block; yes.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible alternative interpretation: Operation 1 "divide nums[i] by 2, rounding up to the nearest whole number" could be `math.ceil(nums[i]/2)`, same as `(x+1)//2`. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "at most op1 times, and not more than once per index" maybe op1 and op2 can be applied to same index but "not more than once per index" for each operation, so both can be applied once. yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "Return the minimum possible sum" if operations can be performed any number but at most op1/op2; yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k but half>=k and final = half-k; if half-k maybe larger than op2 first final? We take min. But if op2 first final smaller, choose op2 first. Is op2 first final ever smaller? We proved no when op1 first valid, but min safe. Let's test x=6,k=4: half3<4 invalid, op2 first final1. x=7,k=4: half4>=4 final0, op2 first final2. min0. x=8,k=5: half4<5 invalid, op2 first final2. x=9,k=5: half5>=5 final0, op2 first final2. min0. x=10,k=5: half5>=5 final0, op2 first final3. min0. Seems op1 first valid gives 0 often. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "op1" option if x=0, v1=0; if op1 budget used, no effect. no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op2" option if x>=k and k=0, v2=x; no effect. no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k and k=0, best = (x+1)//2; half>=0, final2 = half -0 = half; same. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "min DP" if `cur + x` maybe > inf? no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "dp" update if multiple options lead to same state; we take min. good.

Let's draft final code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minArraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int, op1: int, op2: int) -> int:
        INF = 10**18
        dp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
        dp[0][0] = 0

        for x in nums:
            ndp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
            for u1 in range(op1 + 1):
                for u2 in range(op2 + 1):
                    cur = dp[u1][u2]
                    if cur == INF:
                        continue

                    # Do nothing
                    if cur + x < ndp[u1][u2]:
                        ndp[u1][u2] = cur + x

                    # Operation 1
                    if u1 < op1:
                        v = (x + 1) // 2
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1 + 1][u2]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2] = cur + v

                    # Operation 2
                    if u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        v = x - k
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1][u2 + 1] = cur + v

                    # Both operations on this index
                    if u1 < op1 and u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        # Op2 first, then Op1
                        best = (x - k + 1) // 2
                        # Op1 first, then Op2 (if valid)
                        half = (x + 1) // 2
                        if half >= k:
                            final_after_op1_then_op2 = half - k
                            if final_after_op1_then_op2 < best:
                                best = final_after_op1_then_op2
                        if cur + best < ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1] = cur + best

            dp = ndp

        return min(min(row) for row in dp)
```

Example2 should return 3. Let's manually run min DP quickly:

After 4:
From (0,0)=2: none6, op1 4, op2 3, both? x>=3, best op2 first1, op1 first2<3 invalid =>1, state(1,1)=3.
From (1,0)=1: none5, op1? u1=1<2 yes v2 ->3 state(2,0)=3, op2? 1+1=2 state(1,1)=2, both? u1<2,u2<1 yes best1 ->2 state(2,1)=2.
So after 4, dp[2,1]=2 (2 op1? Wait state u1=2,u2=1 sum2 from old(1,0)=1 + both on4 final1 =2. That uses op1 on2 and both on4, op2 used. Then process 3 with no budgets? u1=2,u2=1, none ->5. But optimal uses op1 on2, op1 on4, op2 on3 sum3. Let's see after 4, dp[2,0]=3 (2 op1 +4 op1 =1+2=3). After 3, from dp[2,0]=3, op2 on3 (0) ->3 state(2,1). Answer3. good.

Let's draft final code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minArraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int, op1: int, op2: int) -> int:
        INF = 10**18
        dp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
        dp[0][0] = 0

        for x in nums:
            ndp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
            for u1 in range(op1 + 1):
                for u2 in range(op2 + 1):
                    cur = dp[u1][u2]
                    if cur == INF:
                        continue

                    # Do nothing
                    if cur + x < ndp[u1][u2]:
                        ndp[u1][u2] = cur + x

                    # Operation 1
                    if u1 < op1:
                        v = (x + 1) // 2
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1 + 1][u2]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2] = cur + v

                    # Operation 2
                    if u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        v = x - k
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1][u2 + 1] = cur + v

                    # Both operations on this index
                    if u1 < op1 and u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        # Op2 first, then Op1
                        best = (x - k + 1) // 2
                        # Op1 first, then Op2 (if valid)
                        half = (x + 1) // 2
                        if half >= k:
                            final_after_op1_then_op2 = half - k
                            if final_after_op1_then_op2 < best:
                                best = final_after_op1_then_op2
                        if cur + best < ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1] = cur + best

            dp = ndp

        return min(min(row) for row in dp)
```

Let's draft final code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minArraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int, op1: int, op2: int) -> int:
        INF = 10**18
        dp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
        dp[0][0] = 0

        for x in nums:
            ndp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
            for u1 in range(op1 + 1):
                for u2 in range(op2 + 1):
                    cur = dp[u1][u2]
                    if cur == INF:
                        continue

                    # Do nothing
                    if cur + x < ndp[u1][u2]:
                        ndp[u1][u2] = cur + x

                    # Operation 1
                    if u1 < op1:
                        v = (x + 1) // 2
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1 + 1][u2]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2] = cur + v

                    # Operation 2
                    if u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        v = x - k
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1][u2 + 1] = cur + v

                    # Both operations on this index
                    if u1 < op1 and u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        # Op2 first, then Op1
                        best = (x - k + 1) // 2
                        # Op1 first, then Op2 (if valid)
                        half = (x + 1) // 2
                        if half >= k:
                            final_after_op1_then_op2 = half - k
                            if final_after_op1_then_op2 < best:
                                best = final_after_op1_then_op2
                        if cur + best < ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1] = cur + best

            dp = ndp

        return min(min(row) for row in dp)
```

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1" and "op2" budgets if op1/op2 are not enough to process all; DP handles.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k but op2 first final maybe not minimal if op1 first valid but op1 first final uses op2 after op1; if op1 first valid, op2 condition half>=k. yes.

But if op2 first final smaller, choose op2 first. Is op2 first final ever smaller? We proved no when op1 first valid, but min safe. Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op2" option if x>=k and k=0, v2=x; no effect. no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k and k=0, best = (x+1)//2; half>=0, final2 = half -0 = half; same. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "opts" approach vs explicit transitions. We'll use explicit transitions in code for clarity.

Let's draft final code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minArraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int, op1: int, op2: int) -> int:
        INF = 10**18
        dp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
        dp[0][0] = 0

        for x in nums:
            ndp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
            for u1 in range(op1 + 1):
                for u2 in range(op2 + 1):
                    cur = dp[u1][u2]
                    if cur == INF:
                        continue

                    # Do nothing
                    if cur + x < ndp[u1][u2]:
                        ndp[u1][u2] = cur + x

                    # Operation 1
                    if u1 < op1:
                        v = (x + 1) // 2
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1 + 1][u2]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2] = cur + v

                    # Operation 2
                    if u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        v = x - k
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1][u2 + 1] = cur + v

                    # Both operations on this index
                    if u1 < op1 and u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        # Op2 first, then Op1
                        best = (x - k + 1) // 2
                        # Op1 first, then Op2 (if valid)
                        half = (x + 1) // 2
                        if half >= k:
                            final_after_op1_then_op2 = half - k
                            if final_after_op1_then_op2 < best:
                                best = final_after_op1_then_op2
                        if cur + best < ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1] = cur + best

            dp = ndp

        return min(min(row) for row in dp)
```

Let's draft final code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minArraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int, op1: int, op2: int) -> int:
        INF = 10**18
        dp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
        dp[0][0] = 0

        for x in nums:
            ndp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
            for u1 in range(op1 + 1):
                for u2 in range(op2 + 1):
                    cur = dp[u1][u2]
                    if cur == INF:
                        continue

                    # Do nothing
                    if cur + x < ndp[u1][u2]:
                        ndp[u1][u2] = cur + x

                    # Operation 1
                    if u1 < op1:
                        v = (x + 1) // 2
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1 + 1][u2]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2] = cur + v

                    # Operation 2
                    if u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        v = x - k
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1][u2 + 1] = cur + v

                    # Both operations on this index
                    if u1 < op1 and u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        # Op2 first, then Op1
                        best = (x - k + 1) // 2
                        # Op1 first, then Op2 (if valid)
                        half = (x + 1) // 2
                        if half >= k:
                            final_after_op1_then_op2 = half - k
                            if final_after_op1_then_op2 < best:
                                best = final_after_op1_then_op2
                        if cur + best < ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1] = cur + best

            dp = ndp

        return min(min(row) for row in dp)
```

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k but half<k, only op2 first. We choose best_final initialized to op2 first; half>=k false, so remains. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "both" option if x<k, we skip. But what if k=0 and x<0 impossible. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "op2" option if x>=k but k=0, v2=x, same as none. no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1" option if x=0, v1=0. no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "dp" initialization with `inf = 10**18` and using `cur == inf` skip. If a valid sum could equal 10**18? total max 1e7, no. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "min(min(row) for row in dp)" if dp is list of lists and some rows all inf? dp[0][0] finite. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "nums" list maybe empty? constraints length >=1. no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1, op2" maybe not ints? no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "k" maybe not int? no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "Operation 2" if k is 0 and x>=0, subtract0; if we include op2 option, it can create state with used op2 and same sum. But min DP min over states with fewer used budgets will dominate. However, could it affect future because dp state with used op2 and same sum might be the only way to reach a state with used op2 count exactly some value, but since at most budgets, we don't need exact. But we take min over all states at end. For transitions, a state with unused op2 and same sum can do everything the used-op2 state can do plus more, so the used-op2 state is never needed for optimal answer. But including it doesn't hurt. It might make `min(min(row))` still correct. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1" option if x=0, similar.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k and final same as op2 only, consuming op1. no harm.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "min DP" if we don't consider "none" option for an index but then no operations; we include none. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1" and "op2" budgets "at most" not "exactly"; min over all states handles. If we only considered exact, might require using all budgets, wrong. We don't.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "op1" and "op2" operations can be applied to same index in either order; our both option chooses best final. But what if the best final order uses op1 first, but op2 first would have allowed op1 to reduce a smaller number and maybe op2 reduction k is same; final op1 first smaller. We choose. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "rounding up" after op2 first if x-k is negative? no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "nums[i] greater than or equal to k" for op2 after op1 if half>=k. yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "Operation 1" can be applied to an index with value 0 and "rounding up" 0. yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "Operation 2" can be applied to an index with value 0 and k=0; subtract0. yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1" and "op2" counts if op1/op2 are 0 and both option condition `u1 < op1 and u2 < op2` false. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "min DP" if `op1` or `op2` is large but `nums` length small; dp dimensions large but okay. constraints <=100.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "from typing import List" in code block; yes.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible alternative interpretation: Operation 1 "divide nums[i] by 2, rounding up to the nearest whole number" could be `math.ceil(nums[i]/2)`, same as `(x+1)//2`. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "at most op1 times, and not more than once per index" maybe op1 and op2 can be applied to same index but "not more than once per index" for each operation, so both can be applied once. yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "Return the minimum possible sum" if operations can be performed any number but at most op1/op2; yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "nums" values up to 1e5, sum up to 1e7, inf 1e18 safe. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k but op2 first final maybe not minimal if op1 first valid but op1 first final uses op2 after op1; if op1 first valid, op2 condition half>=k. yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k but half>=k and final = half-k; if half-k maybe larger than op2 first final? We take min. But if op2 first final smaller, choose op2 first. Is op2 first final ever smaller? We proved no when op1 first valid, but min safe. Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op2" option if x>=k and k=0, v2=x; no effect. no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k and k=0, best = (x+1)//2; half>=0, final2 = half -0 = half; same. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "min DP" if `cur + x` maybe > inf? no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "opts" approach vs explicit transitions. We'll use explicit transitions in code for clarity.

Let's draft final code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minArraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int, op1: int, op2: int) -> int:
        INF = 10**18
        dp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
        dp[0][0] = 0

        for x in nums:
            ndp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
            for u1 in range(op1 + 1):
                for u2 in range(op2 + 1):
                    cur = dp[u1][u2]
                    if cur == INF:
                        continue

                    # Do nothing
                    if cur + x < ndp[u1][u2]:
                        ndp[u1][u2] = cur + x

                    # Operation 1
                    if u1 < op1:
                        v = (x + 1) // 2
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1 + 1][u2]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2] = cur + v

                    # Operation 2
                    if u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        v = x - k
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1][u2 + 1] = cur + v

                    # Both operations on this index
                    if u1 < op1 and u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        # Op2 first, then Op1
                        best = (x - k + 1) // 2
                        # Op1 first, then Op2 (if valid)
                        half = (x + 1) // 2
                        if half >= k:
                            final_after_op1_then_op2 = half - k
                            if final_after_op1_then_op2 < best:
                                best = final_after_op1_then_op2
                        if cur + best < ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1] = cur + best

            dp = ndp

        return min(min(row) for row in dp)
```

After 4:
From (0,0)=2: none6, op1 4, op2 3, both? x>=3, best op2 first1, op1 first2<3 invalid =>1, state(1,1)=3.

So after 4, dp[2,1]=2 (2 op1? Wait state u1=2,u2=1 sum2 from old(1,0)=1 + both on4 final1 =2. That uses op1 on2 and both on4, op2 used. Then process 3 with no budgets? u1=2,u2=1, none ->5. But optimal uses op1 on2, op1 on4, op2 on3 sum3. Let's see after 4, dp[2,0]=3 (2 op1 +4 op1 =1+2=3). After 3, from dp[2,0]=3, op2 on3 (0) ->3 state(2,1). Answer3. good.

Let's draft final code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minArraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int, op1: int, op2: int) -> int:
        INF = 10**18
        dp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
        dp[0][0] = 0

        for x in nums:
            ndp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
            for u1 in range(op1 + 1):
                for u2 in range(op2 + 1):
                    cur = dp[u1][u2]
                    if cur == INF:
                        continue

                    # Do nothing
                    if cur + x < ndp[u1][u2]:
                        ndp[u1][u2] = cur + x

                    # Operation 1
                    if u1 < op1:
                        v = (x + 1) // 2
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1 + 1][u2]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2] = cur + v

                    # Operation 2
                    if u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        v = x - k
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1][u2 + 1] = cur + v

                    # Both operations on this index
                    if u1 < op1 and u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        # Op2 first, then Op1
                        best = (x - k + 1) // 2
                        # Op1 first, then Op2 (if valid)
                        half = (x + 1) // 2
                        if half >= k:
                            final_after_op1_then_op2 = half - k
                            if final_after_op1_then_op2 < best:
                                best = final_after_op1_then_op2
                        if cur + best < ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1] = cur + best

            dp = ndp

        return min(min(row) for row in dp)
```

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "Operation 2" if x>=k but after op1 first half>=k, final = half-k; if half-k maybe not integer? half integer. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "Operation 1" if x after op2 is 0, v=(0+1)//2=0. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "Operation 2" if x after op1 is 0 and k=0, v=0. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op2" option if x>=k but x-k maybe 0, v=0. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k and op2 first final maybe 0, op1 first invalid. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "min DP" if a state with used op1/op2 and lower sum is not necessarily reachable with the same set of processed items? It is reachable by some choices; DP stores min. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "dp" update order: We use `ndp` initialized INF, and for each state we update. We don't use `ndp` in same iteration, so no item reused. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "op1" and "op2" budgets if op1/op2 are less than number of items; dp dimensions smaller. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "min DP" if `op1` or `op2` is 0 and `u1 < op1` false, so no op1. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "both" option if `u1 < op1 and u2 < op2 and x >= k`; if op1 or op2 budget zero, skip. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "Operation 2" if x>=k but u2 < op2; if op2 budget zero, skip. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "Operation 1" if u1 < op1; if op1 budget zero, skip. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "none" option always allowed. yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "cur + x" maybe repeated many times; no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "from typing import List" in code block; yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "final code enclosed in a code block" maybe no explanation. We'll output code block.

Before final, let's think if there is any hidden nuance about "rounding up to the nearest whole number" for division by 2: If x is odd, e.g., 3 -> 2, yes. If x is even, 4 ->2. Formula (x+1)//2 gives for 4:5//2=2. yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "Operation 2: subtract k from nums[i], but only if nums[i] is greater than or equal to k." If k=0, subtract0; if nums[i]>=0. yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "op1, op2 <= nums.length" but if op1=100 and op2=100, dp 101x101; loops 100*10201*4=4,080,400. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "nums[i] <= 10^5" but after op1 and op2 final maybe 0. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "k <= 10^5" but if k > x, op2 invalid. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k but op2 first final = (x-k+1)//2; if x-k is large, okay. If x-k=0, final0. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1 first final = half - k" if half>=k; if half-k maybe 0. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "min DP" if both option final is computed as min of two orders, but if op1 first valid and op2 first valid, the min final is op1 first. But what if op1 first valid but op2 first final is also valid and maybe applying op2 first then op1 uses op1 on a smaller value, but op1 first then op2 uses op2 on a larger value. Both consume same budgets. We choose min final. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op2 first final" formula if x>=k but op2 first result x-k, then op1 ceil((x-k)/2). yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1 first final" formula if half>=k, then op2 subtract k. yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k and half>=k, but op1 first final maybe less than op2 first final. We choose min. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k but half<k, only op2 first. We choose best_final initialized to op2 first; half>=k false, so remains. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "both" option if x<k, we skip. But what if k=0 and x<0 impossible. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "op2" option if x>=k but k=0, v2=x, same as none. no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1" option if x=0, v1=0. no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "dp" initialization with `inf = 10**18` and using `cur == inf` skip. If a valid sum could equal 10**18? total max 1e7, no. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "min(min(row) for row in dp)" if dp is list of lists and some rows all inf? dp[0][0] finite. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "nums" list maybe empty? constraints length >=1. no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1, op2" maybe not ints? no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "k" maybe not int? no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "Operation 2" if k is 0 and x>=0, subtract0; if we include op2 option, it can create state with used op2 and same sum. But min DP min over states with fewer used budgets will dominate. However, could it affect future because dp state with used op2 and same sum might be the only way to reach a state with used op2 count exactly some value, but since at most budgets, we don't need exact. But we take min over all states at end. For transitions, a state with unused op2 and same sum can do everything the used-op2 state can do plus more, so the used-op2 state is never needed for optimal answer. But including it doesn't hurt. It might make `min(min(row))` still correct. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1" option if x=0, similar.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k and final same as op2 only, consuming op1. no harm.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "min DP" if we don't consider "none" option for an index but then no operations; we include none. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1" and "op2" budgets "at most" not "exactly"; min over all states handles. If we only considered exact, might require using all budgets, wrong. We don't.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "op1" and "op2" operations can be applied to same index in either order; our both option chooses best final. But what if the best final order uses op1 first, but op2 first would have allowed op1 to reduce a smaller number and maybe op2 reduction k is same; final op1 first smaller. We choose. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "rounding up" after op2 first if x-k is negative? no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "nums[i] greater than or equal to k" for op2 after op1 if half>=k. yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "Operation 1" can be applied to an index with value 0 and "rounding up" 0. yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "Operation 2" can be applied to an index with value 0 and k=0; subtract0. yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1" and "op2" counts if op1/op2 are 0 and both option condition `u1 < op1 and u2 < op2` false. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "min DP" if `op1` or `op2` is large but `nums` length small; dp dimensions large but okay. constraints <=100.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "from typing import List" in code block; yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "Operation 2: subtract k from nums[i], but only if nums[i] is greater than or equal to k." If k=0, subtract0; if nums[i]>=0. yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "at most op1 times, and not more than once per index" maybe op1 and op2 can be applied to same index but "not more than once per index" for each operation, so both can be applied once. yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "Return the minimum possible sum" if operations can be performed any number but at most op1/op2; yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "nums" values up to 1e5, sum up to 1e7, inf 1e18 safe. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k but op2 first final maybe not minimal if op1 first valid but op1 first final uses op2 after op1; if op1 first valid, op2 condition half>=k. yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k but half>=k and final = half-k; if half-k maybe larger than op2 first final? We take min. But if op2 first final smaller, choose op2 first. Is op2 first final ever smaller? We proved no when op1 first valid, but min safe. Let's test x=6,k=4: half3<4 invalid, op2 first final1. x=7,k=4: half4>=4 final0, op2 first final2. min0. x=8,k=5: half4<5 invalid, op2 first final2. x=9,k=5: half5>=5 final0, op2 first final2. min0. x=10,k=5: half5>=5 final0, op2 first final3. min0. Seems op1 first valid gives 0 often. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "op1" option if x=0, v1=0; if op1 budget used, no effect. no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op2" option if x>=k and k=0, v2=x; no effect. no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k and k=0, best = (x+1)//2; half>=0, final2 = half -0 = half; same. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "min DP" if `cur + x` maybe > inf? no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "dp" update if multiple options lead to same state; we take min. good.
