Let's draft final code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minArraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int, op1: int, op2: int) -> int:
        INF = 10**18
        dp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
        dp[0][0] = 0

        for x in nums:
            ndp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
            for u1 in range(op1 + 1):
                for u2 in range(op2 + 1):
                    cur = dp[u1][u2]
                    if cur == INF:
                        continue

                    # Do nothing
                    if cur + x < ndp[u1][u2]:
                        ndp[u1][u2] = cur + x

                    # Operation 1
                    if u1 < op1:
                        v = (x + 1) // 2
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1 + 1][u2]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2] = cur + v

                    # Operation 2
                    if u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        v = x - k
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1][u2 + 1] = cur + v

                    # Both operations on this index
                    if u1 < op1 and u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        # Op2 first, then Op1
                        best = (x - k + 1) // 2
                        # Op1 first, then Op2 (if valid)
                        half = (x + 1) // 2
                        if half >= k:
                            final_after_op1_then_op2 = half - k
                            if final_after_op1_then_op2 < best:
                                best = final_after_op1_then_op2
                        if cur + best < ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1] = cur + best

            dp = ndp

        return min(min(row) for row in dp)
```

Example2 should return 3. Let's manually run min DP quickly:

After 2:
dp[0][0]=2, dp[1][0]=1, dp[0][1]=inf, dp[1][1]=inf, dp[2][0]? op1 from (1,0) none? Actually process one item, dp[2][0] inf because cannot use op1 twice on same index. good.
After 4:
From (0,0)=2: none6, op1 4, op2 3, both? x>=3, best op2 first1, op1 first2<3 invalid =>1, state(1,1)=3.
From (1,0)=1: none5, op1? u1=1<2 yes v2 ->3 state(2,0)=3, op2? 1+1=2 state(1,1)=2, both? u1<2,u2<1 yes best1 ->2 state(2,1)=2.
So after 4, dp[2,1]=2 (2 op1? Wait state u1=2,u2=1 sum2 from old(1,0)=1 + both on4 final1 =2. That uses op1 on2 and both on4, op2 used. Then process 3 with no budgets? u1=2,u2=1, none ->5. But optimal uses op1 on2, op1 on4, op2 on3 sum3. Let's see after 4, dp[2,0]=3 (2 op1 +4 op1 =1+2=3). After 3, from dp[2,0]=3, op2 on3 (0) ->3 state(2,1). Answer3. good.

Let's draft final code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minArraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int, op1: int, op2: int) -> int:
        INF = 10**18
        dp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
        dp[0][0] = 0

        for x in nums:
            ndp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
            for u1 in range(op1 + 1):
                for u2 in range(op2 + 1):
                    cur = dp[u1][u2]
                    if cur == INF:
                        continue

                    # Do nothing
                    if cur + x < ndp[u1][u2]:
                        ndp[u1][u2] = cur + x

                    # Operation 1
                    if u1 < op1:
                        v = (x + 1) // 2
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1 + 1][u2]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2] = cur + v

                    # Operation 2
                    if u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        v = x - k
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1][u2 + 1] = cur + v

                    # Both operations on this index
                    if u1 < op1 and u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        # Op2 first, then Op1
                        best = (x - k + 1) // 2
                        # Op1 first, then Op2 (if valid)
                        half = (x + 1) // 2
                        if half >= k:
                            final_after_op1_then_op2 = half - k
                            if final_after_op1_then_op2 < best:
                                best = final_after_op1_then_op2
                        if cur + best < ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1] = cur + best

            dp = ndp

        return min(min(row) for row in dp)
```

Let's draft final code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minArraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int, op1: int, op2: int) -> int:
        INF = 10**18
        dp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
        dp[0][0] = 0

        for x in nums:
            ndp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
            for u1 in range(op1 + 1):
                for u2 in range(op2 + 1):
                    cur = dp[u1][u2]
                    if cur == INF:
                        continue

                    # Do nothing
                    if cur + x < ndp[u1][u2]:
                        ndp[u1][u2] = cur + x

                    # Operation 1
                    if u1 < op1:
                        v = (x + 1) // 2
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1 + 1][u2]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2] = cur + v

                    # Operation 2
                    if u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        v = x - k
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1][u2 + 1] = cur + v

                    # Both operations on this index
                    if u1 < op1 and u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        # Op2 first, then Op1
                        best = (x - k + 1) // 2
                        # Op1 first, then Op2 (if valid)
                        half = (x + 1) // 2
                        if half >= k:
                            final_after_op1_then_op2 = half - k
                            if final_after_op1_then_op2 < best:
                                best = final_after_op1_then_op2
                        if cur + best < ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1] = cur + best

            dp = ndp

        return min(min(row) for row in dp)
```

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op2" option if x>=k and k=0, v2=x; no effect. no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k and k=0, best = (x+1)//2; half>=0, final2 = half -0 = half; same. good.

Let's draft final code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minArraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int, op1: int, op2: int) -> int:
        INF = 10**18
        dp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
        dp[0][0] = 0

        for x in nums:
            ndp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
            for u1 in range(op1 + 1):
                for u2 in range(op2 + 1):
                    cur = dp[u1][u2]
                    if cur == INF:
                        continue

                    # Do nothing
                    if cur + x < ndp[u1][u2]:
                        ndp[u1][u2] = cur + x

                    # Operation 1
                    if u1 < op1:
                        v = (x + 1) // 2
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1 + 1][u2]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2] = cur + v

                    # Operation 2
                    if u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        v = x - k
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1][u2 + 1] = cur + v

                    # Both operations on this index
                    if u1 < op1 and u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        # Op2 first, then Op1
                        best = (x - k + 1) // 2
                        # Op1 first, then Op2 (if valid)
                        half = (x + 1) // 2
                        if half >= k:
                            final_after_op1_then_op2 = half - k
                            if final_after_op1_then_op2 < best:
                                best = final_after_op1_then_op2
                        if cur + best < ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1] = cur + best

            dp = ndp

        return min(min(row) for row in dp)
```

Let's draft final code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minArraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int, op1: int, op2: int) -> int:
        INF = 10**18
        dp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
        dp[0][0] = 0

        for x in nums:
            ndp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
            for u1 in range(op1 + 1):
                for u2 in range(op2 + 1):
                    cur = dp[u1][u2]
                    if cur == INF:
                        continue

                    # Do nothing
                    if cur + x < ndp[u1][u2]:
                        ndp[u1][u2] = cur + x

                    # Operation 1
                    if u1 < op1:
                        v = (x + 1) // 2
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1 + 1][u2]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2] = cur + v

                    # Operation 2
                    if u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        v = x - k
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1][u2 + 1] = cur + v

                    # Both operations on this index
                    if u1 < op1 and u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        # Op2 first, then Op1
                        best = (x - k + 1) // 2
                        # Op1 first, then Op2 (if valid)
                        half = (x + 1) // 2
                        if half >= k:
                            final_after_op1_then_op2 = half - k
                            if final_after_op1_then_op2 < best:
                                best = final_after_op1_then_op2
                        if cur + best < ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1] = cur + best

            dp = ndp

        return min(min(row) for row in dp)
```

But what if op1 first valid but op2 first final is also valid and maybe applying op2 first then op1 uses op1 on a smaller value, but op1 first then op2 uses op2 on a larger value. Both consume same budgets. We choose min final. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op2 first final" formula if x>=k but op2 first result x-k, then op1 ceil((x-k)/2). yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1 first final" formula if half>=k, then op2 subtract k. yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k and half>=k, but op1 first final maybe less than op2 first final. We choose min. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k but half<k, only op2 first. We choose best_final initialized to op2 first; half>=k false, so remains. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "both" option if x<k, we skip. But what if k=0 and x<0 impossible. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "op2" option if x>=k but k=0, v2=x, same as none. no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1" option if x=0, v1=0. no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "dp" initialization with `inf = 10**18` and using `cur == inf` skip. If a valid sum could equal 10**18? total max 1e7, no. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "min(min(row) for row in dp)" if dp is list of lists and some rows all inf? dp[0][0] finite. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "nums" list maybe empty? constraints length >=1. no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1, op2" maybe not ints? no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "k" maybe not int? no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "Operation 2" if k is 0 and x>=0, subtract0; if we include op2 option, it can create state with used op2 and same sum. But min DP min over states with fewer used budgets will dominate. However, could it affect future because dp state with used op2 and same sum might be the only way to reach a state with used op2 count exactly some value, but since at most budgets, we don't need exact. But we take min over all states at end. For transitions, a state with unused op2 and same sum can do everything the used-op2 state can do plus more, so the used-op2 state is never needed for optimal answer. But including it doesn't hurt. It might make `min(min(row))` still correct. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1" option if x=0, similar.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k and final same as op2 only, consuming op1. no harm.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "min DP" if we don't consider "none" option for an index but then no operations; we include none. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1" and "op2" budgets "at most" not "exactly"; min over all states handles. If we only considered exact, might require using all budgets, wrong. We don't.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "op1" and "op2" operations can be applied to same index in either order; our both option chooses best final. But what if the best final order uses op1 first, but op2 first would have allowed op1 to reduce a smaller number and maybe op2 reduction k is same; final op1 first smaller. We choose. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "rounding up" after op2 first if x-k is negative? no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "nums[i] greater than or equal to k" for op2 after op1 if half>=k. yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "Operation 1" can be applied to an index with value 0 and "rounding up" 0. yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "Operation 2" can be applied to an index with value 0 and k=0; subtract0. yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1" and "op2" counts if op1/op2 are 0 and both option condition `u1 < op1 and u2 < op2` false. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "min DP" if `op1` or `op2` is large but `nums` length small; dp dimensions large but okay. constraints <=100.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "from typing import List" in code block; yes.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible alternative interpretation: Operation 1 "divide nums[i] by 2, rounding up to the nearest whole number" could be `math.ceil(nums[i]/2)`, same as `(x+1)//2`. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "Operation 2: subtract k from nums[i], but only if nums[i] is greater than or equal to k." If k=0, subtract0; if nums[i]>=0. yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "at most op1 times, and not more than once per index" maybe op1 and op2 can be applied to same index but "not more than once per index" for each operation, so both can be applied once. yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "Return the minimum possible sum" if operations can be performed any number but at most op1/op2; yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "nums" values up to 1e5, sum up to 1e7, inf 1e18 safe. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op1" and "op2" budgets if op1/op2 are not enough to process all; DP handles.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k but op2 first final maybe not minimal if op1 first valid but op1 first final uses op2 after op1; if op1 first valid, op2 condition half>=k. yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k but half>=k and final = half-k; if half-k maybe larger than op2 first final? We take min. But if op2 first final smaller, choose op2 first. Is op2 first final ever smaller? We proved no when op1 first valid, but min safe. Let's test x=6,k=4: half3<4 invalid, op2 first final1. x=7,k=4: half4>=4 final0, op2 first final2. min0. x=8,k=5: half4<5 invalid, op2 first final2. x=9,k=5: half5>=5 final0, op2 first final2. min0. x=10,k=5: half5>=5 final0, op2 first final3. min0. Seems op1 first valid gives 0 often. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k and half>=k but final = half-k; if half-k maybe less than op2 first final. yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "op2 first final" formula if x-k is odd/even; yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "min DP" if we don't use `math.ceil`, use integer formula. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "op1" option if x=0, v1=0; if op1 budget used, no effect. no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "op2" option if x>=k and k=0, v2=x; no effect. no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "both" option if x>=k and k=0, best = (x+1)//2; half>=0, final2 = half -0 = half; same. good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "min DP" if `cur + x` maybe > inf? no.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "dp" update if multiple options lead to same state; we take min. good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "opts" approach vs explicit transitions. We'll use explicit transitions in code for clarity.

Let's draft final code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minArraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int, op1: int, op2: int) -> int:
        INF = 10**18
        dp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
        dp[0][0] = 0

        for x in nums:
            ndp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
            for u1 in range(op1 + 1):
                for u2 in range(op2 + 1):
                    cur = dp[u1][u2]
                    if cur == INF:
                        continue

                    # Do nothing
                    if cur + x < ndp[u1][u2]:
                        ndp[u1][u2] = cur + x

                    # Operation 1
                    if u1 < op1:
                        v = (x + 1) // 2
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1 + 1][u2]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2] = cur + v

                    # Operation 2
                    if u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        v = x - k
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1][u2 + 1] = cur + v

                    # Both operations on this index
                    if u1 < op1 and u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        # Op2 first, then Op1
                        best = (x - k + 1) // 2
                        # Op1 first, then Op2 (if valid)
                        half = (x + 1) // 2
                        if half >= k:
                            final_after_op1_then_op2 = half - k
                            if final_after_op1_then_op2 < best:
                                best = final_after_op1_then_op2
                        if cur + best < ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1] = cur + best

            dp = ndp

        return min(min(row) for row in dp)
```

After 4:
From (0,0)=2: none6, op1 4, op2 3, both? x>=3, best op2 first1, op1 first2<3 invalid =>1, state(1,1)=3.
From (1,0)=1: none5, op1? u1=1<2 yes v2 ->3 state(2,0)=3, op2? 1+1=2 state(1,1)=2, both? u1<2,u2<1 yes best1 ->2 state(2,1)=2.
So after 4, dp[2,1]=2 (2 op1? Wait state u1=2,u2=1 sum2 from old(1,0)=1 + both on4 final1 =2. That uses op1 on2 and both on4, op2 used. Then process 3 with no budgets? u1=2,u2=1, none ->5. But optimal uses op1 on2, op1 on4, op2 on3 sum3. Let's see after 4, dp[2,0]=3 (2 op1 +4 op1 =1+2=3). After 3, from dp[2,0]=3, op2 on3 (0) ->3 state(2,1). Answer3. good.

Let's draft final code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minArraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int, op1: int, op2: int) -> int:
        INF = 10**18
        dp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
        dp[0][0] = 0

        for x in nums:
            ndp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
            for u1 in range(op1 + 1):
                for u2 in range(op2 + 1):
                    cur = dp[u1][u2]
                    if cur == INF:
                        continue

                    # Do nothing
                    if cur + x < ndp[u1][u2]:
                        ndp[u1][u2] = cur + x

                    # Operation 1
                    if u1 < op1:
                        v = (x + 1) // 2
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1 + 1][u2]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2] = cur + v

                    # Operation 2
                    if u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        v = x - k
                        if cur + v < ndp[u1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1][u2 + 1] = cur + v

                    # Both operations on this index
                    if u1 < op1 and u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        # Op2 first, then Op1
                        best = (x - k + 1) // 2
                        # Op1 first, then Op2 (if valid)
                        half = (x + 1) // 2
                        if half >= k:
                            final_after_op1_then_op2 = half - k
                            if final_after_op1_then_op2 < best:
                                best = final_after_op1_then_op2
                        if cur + best < ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1]:
                            ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1] = cur + best

            dp = ndp

        return min(min(row) for row in dp)
```

=== ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minArraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int, op1: int, op2: int) -> int:
        INF = 10**18
        dp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]
        dp[0][0] = 0

        for x in nums:
            ndp = [[INF] * (op2 + 1) for _ in range(op1 + 1)]

            for u1 in range(op1 + 1):
                for u2 in range(op2 + 1):
                    cur = dp[u1][u2]
                    if cur == INF:
                        continue

                    # Do nothing
                    ndp[u1][u2] = min(ndp[u1][u2], cur + x)

                    # Operation 1
                    if u1 < op1:
                        v = (x + 1) // 2
                        ndp[u1 + 1][u2] = min(ndp[u1 + 1][u2], cur + v)

                    # Operation 2
                    if u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        v = x - k
                        ndp[u1][u2 + 1] = min(ndp[u1][u2 + 1], cur + v)

                    # Both operations on the same index
                    if u1 < op1 and u2 < op2 and x >= k:
                        # Op2 first, then Op1
                        best = (x - k + 1) // 2

                        # Op1 first, then Op2, if valid
                        half = (x + 1) // 2
                        if half >= k:
                            best = min(best, half - k)

                        ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1] = min(ndp[u1 + 1][u2 + 1], cur + best)

            dp = ndp

        return min(min(row) for row in dp)
```
